People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Around when my wife’s health problems got bad and everything at home changed. It’s been… about a year and seven months, since my wife’s health got bad and things at home changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “depressed” feels like a big word. I’ve been pretty low a lot, I guess. Most days, yeah.\n\nDoctor: If your wife’s health problems had not happened, do you think you would still be feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean… it really started when her health got bad and everything at home changed. I don’t really know how to answer that neatly, but it hasn’t exactly gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any stretches during this time when your mood and loss of interest went back to normal for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: No… not really. It’s been kind of continuous since all that happened at home. About a year and seven months now, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you had trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Or I’ll wake up at three or four and just stare at the ceiling until I have to leave.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Pretty much all the time. By midmorning I feel like I’ve already done a whole shift… even just walking the site or taking stairs, it feels like I’m dragging wet concrete around.\n\nDoctor: Has this been interfering with your work or your ability to take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. My wife and kids kind of have to work around me now instead of being able to count on me like before.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than what you would expect just from what’s been happening with your wife’s health?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean… her health has been a lot, so it’s hard to separate it out. In meetings I’ll start to say something and just… lose it. And decisions I used to make in five minutes, I’ll just sit there stuck.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or like you’ve been letting your family down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do.\n\nI keep feeling like I’m failing everybody. My wife, the kids… the guys at work. If something goes wrong, even something small, I take it like… there it is, proof I shouldn’t be the one people are depending on. By midmorning I feel like I’ve already done a whole shift… even just walking the site or taking stairs, it feels like I’m dragging wet concrete around.\n\nDoctor: Has this been interfering with your work or your ability to take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. Work especially. I’ve been missing things I shouldn’t miss—stuff that can throw off deadlines, and… safety things. There was a near-miss, and after that I couldn’t really keep telling myself it was just normal stress.\n\nAt home, I’m there, but not really useful. My wife and kids kind of have to work around me now instead of being able to count on me like before.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than what you would expect just from what’s been happening with your wife’s health?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean… her health has been a lot, so it’s hard to separate it out. It feels bigger than just that sometimes, but I’m not really sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. More than I’d like to admit.\n\nI used to be able to keep schedules, subs, all the moving parts straight in my head. Now I’ll read the same email three times and still not really take it in. In meetings I’ll start to say something and just… lose it. And decisions I used to make in five minutes, I’ll just sit there stuck.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or like you’ve been letting your family down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do.\n\nI keep feeling like I’m failing everybody. My wife, the kids… the guys at work. If something goes wrong, even something small, I take it like… there it is, proof I shouldn’t be the one people are depending on. Like I’m just useless in the role.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: …Yeah. I don’t like saying that, but yeah.\n\nOn those early drives, before anyone’s really up, I catch myself thinking it’d be easier if I just… didn’t make it there. And sometimes the idea of steering off the road comes into my head, and it scares me because it feels too close.\n\nDoctor: If your wife’s health suddenly improved and the situation at home settled down, do you think you would still feel this depressed?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know.\n\nI’d like to think that would fix it, or at least help. But I’m not sure it would all just go away. It’s hard to separate what’s the situation and what’s… me, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks in a row during this time when these symptoms were present nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Definitely more than two weeks.\n\nIt’s been… pretty much going on for about a year and seven months now, since my wife’s health problems changed things at home. Most days, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your reaction has been out of proportion to what most people would experience in your situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean… it feels like a lot, but I don’t really know what “most people” would feel in this situation.\n\nMaybe I should be handling it better. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before your wife’s health problems began, had you ever gone through a period like this in your life?\n\nPatient: Not really… not like this.\n\nI’ve had rough patches, sure, but I don’t remember anything that felt this… prolonged, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’ve been pretty low a lot, I guess. Most days, yeah.\n\nDoctor: If your wife’s health problems had not happened, do you think you would still be feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean… it really started when her health got bad and everything at home changed. It’s been about a year and seven months since then, so… it’s hard to say.\n\nDoctor: Are your wife’s health problems still going on?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really know how to answer that neatly, but it hasn’t exactly gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any stretches during this time when your mood and loss of interest went back to normal for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: No… not really. It’s been kind of continuous since all that happened at home. About a year and seven months now, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you had trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean, I’ll be exhausted when I get in bed, but then I just… lie there thinking about bills, schedules, stuff for the job. Or I’ll wake up at three or four and just stare at the ceiling until I have to leave.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Pretty much all the time. By midmorning I feel like I’ve already done a whole shift… even just walking the site or taking stairs, it feels like I’m dragging wet concrete around.\n\nDoctor: Has this been interfering with your work or your ability to take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. Work especially. I’ve been missing things I shouldn’t miss—stuff that can throw off deadlines, and… safety things. There was a near-miss, and after that I couldn’t really keep telling myself it was just normal stress.\n\nAt home, I’m there, but not really useful. My wife and kids kind of have to work around me now instead of being able to count on me like before.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than what you would expect just from what’s been happening with your wife’s health?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean… her health has been a lot, so it’s hard to separate it out. It feels bigger than just that sometimes, but I’m not really sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. More than I’d like to admit.\n\nI used to be able to keep schedules, subs, all the moving parts straight in my head. Now I’ll read the same email three times and still not really take it in. In meetings I’ll start to say something and just… lose it. And decisions I used to make in five minutes, I’ll just sit there stuck.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or like you’ve been letting your family down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do.\n\nI keep feeling like I’m failing everybody. My wife, the kids… the guys at work. If something goes wrong, even something small, I take it like… there it is, proof I shouldn’t be the one people are depending on. Like I’m just useless in the role.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: …Yeah. I don’t like saying that, but yeah.\n\nOn those early drives, before anyone’s really up, I catch myself thinking it’d be easier if I just… didn’t make it there. And sometimes the idea of steering off the road comes into my head, and it scares me because it feels too close.\n\nDoctor: If your wife’s health suddenly improved and the situation at home settled down, do you think you would still feel this depressed?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know.\n\nI’d like to think that would fix it, or at least help. But I’m not sure it would all just go away. It’s hard to separate what’s the situation and what’s… me, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks in a row during this time when these symptoms were present nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Definitely more than two weeks.\n\nIt’s been… pretty much going on for about a year and seven months now, since my wife’s health problems changed things at home. Most days, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your reaction has been out of proportion to what most people would experience in your situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean… it feels like a lot, but I don’t really know what “most people” would feel in this situation.\n\nMaybe I should be handling it better. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before your wife’s health problems began, had you ever gone through a period like this in your life?\n\nPatient: Not really… not like this.\n\nI’ve had rough patches, sure, but I don’t remember anything that felt this… prolonged, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you get even a brief break from things at home, does your mood lift?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really something I’ve noticed. Even when I get a little time away, it doesn’t feel like much changes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Like I’m just useless in the role.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: …Yeah. I don’t like saying that, but yeah.\n\nOn those early drives, before anyone’s really up, I catch myself thinking it’d be easier if I just… didn’t make it there. And sometimes the idea of steering off the road comes into my head, and it scares me because it feels too close.\n\nDoctor: If your wife’s health suddenly improved and the situation at home settled down, do you think you would still feel this depressed?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know.\n\nI’d like to think that would fix it, or at least help. But I’m not sure it would all just go away. It’s hard to separate what’s the situation and what’s… me, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks in a row during this time when these symptoms were present nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Definitely more than two weeks.\n\nIt’s been… pretty much going on for about a year and seven months now, since my wife’s health problems changed things at home. Most days, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your reaction has been out of proportion to what most people would experience in your situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean… it feels like a lot, but I don’t really know what “most people” would feel in this situation.\n\nMaybe I should be handling it better. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before your wife’s health problems began, had you ever gone through a period like this in your life?\n\nPatient: Not really… not like this.\n\nI’ve had rough patches, sure, but I don’t remember anything that felt this… prolonged, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you get even a brief break from things at home, does your mood lift?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really something I’ve noticed. Even when I get a little time away, it doesn’t feel like much changes.\n\nDoctor: Before your wife became ill, had you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt this same kind of low mood and loss of interest nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Before all this, I still had things I cared about.\n\nNow it’s more like… I just don’t care about much. My fishing stuff’s been sitting in the garage, I don’t really want to go into the woodshop, and even when the kids want to play chess, I’m just kind of… there. Going through it."
